[...] The HEAT position commands the heating system to warm the vehicle interior to a preset temperature. In the VENT position, the system draws fresh air into the vehicle. The COOL position commands the air conditioning system to cool the vehicle interior to a preset temperature. The OFF position deactivates the system. Dash Fan Switches The RH and LH Dash Fan toggle switches control the dash fans. To run a fan, reposition a switch from OFF to either HIGH or LOW for a desired speed. Speaker Select Switch The Speaker Select toggle switch controls the interior and exterior speakers of the public address (P.A.) system. [...] Insert the belt clip into the belt buckle. 5. Ensure the belt is retracted, locked, and snug. WHEELCHAIR SYSTEM Rev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 11. INTRODUCTION**This manual describes the operating features and safety equipment of the New Flyer transit vehicle. All personnel involved in the operation of the vehicle should be acquainted with this manual and should familiarize themselves with the vehicle, before providing any public service. Knowing the contents of this booklet and following its recommendations will help to assure safe and trouble-free operation. It is not the intention or responsibility of this manual to give instruction in the use of common sense, basic skills and rules of driving; therefore, it is assumed that you, the operator, are fully qualified to operate a public transit vehicle. This manual and any other supplied should be considered a permanent part of the vehicle and remain with the vehicle at all times. The information and specifications throughout this manual are up to date at time of publication. New Flyer reserves the right to change the content of this manual at any time without notice. Any malfunction which interferes with the safe operation of the vehicle should be reported immediately to the appropriate service personnel. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ New Flyer urges you the driver to read this publication carefully, as well as the following manuals which are readily available from the respective manufacturer.\_** \u2022**\_ Voi th\_ Operating Instructions DIWA.5 Transmissions** \u007F**\_ Cummins \_ISL Series Engine Owner\u2019s Manual ![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_7\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_8\u003E\r\n**2\*\* Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\n80,000 Miles (128,000 km) Preventive Maintenance 2.17. 80,000 Miles (128,000 km) Preventive Maintenance 2.17.1. Coolant\*\* Change coolant every 80,000 miles (128,000 km) 2,000 operating hours, or two years. Refer to Section 6 of this manual for the coolant draining and filling procedure.\*\* 2.17.2. Air Compressor Discharge Lines\*\* Inspect the air compressor discharge lines for carbon buildup every every 80,000 miles (128,000 km), 2,000 operating hours or two years. If the total deposit in a crosssection of the line exceeds 1/16\u0022, clean the line, and inspect and clean the air compressor cylinder head and valve assembly. Refer to Section 8 of this manual for further information on this equipment.\*\* 2.18. 96,000 Miles (154,500 km) Preventive Maintenance 2.18.1. Front \u0026 Rear Brake Chambers\*\* Perform these maintenance and overhaul procedures every 96,000 miles (154,500 km) or 12 months. \u2752 Thoroughly clean the exterior of the brake chamber, fittings and air line connections. \u2752 Replace rubber parts such as the dust boot, O-ring or diaphragm that are cracked, hardened, distorted or stretched. Use a new dust boot for final assembly. \u2752 Replace a push rod nylon bushing with center wear over 1.524\u0022 (38.7 mm). \u2752 Check the cylinder bore diameter for rust, scoring or dents. Do not reuse a cylinder with a bore inner diameter measuring over 6.270\u0022 (159.3 mm) after cleaning. \u2752 Inspect cylinders meeting inner diameter tolerance specifications for further wear or damage. Cylinders require replacement if cylinder wall sections are worn thin beyond the maximum permissible inner diameter specification or if the mounting studs are worn or damaged.\r\n\u2752 Remove all nicks or scratches from the piston skirt. \u2752 The piston stem is chrome plated. Pistons with stems in good condition, not corroded or scored, are reusable. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Find out the cause of piston stem corroding or scoring and eliminate this to prevent future piston stem damage.\_ \u2752 After thoroughly inspecting and cleaning the piston and stem, coat with a thin film of WD \u2013 40\u00AE to lubricate and provide antirust protection. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ Apply only enough lubricant to lightly coat the piston stem to provide a protective film.\_ \u261E** NOTE:**\_ DO NOT overspray paint on the piston stem.\_ \u2752 Spray or soak the large spring and spring retainer with a rust remover and inhibitor such as WD \u2013 40\u00AE to loosen and remove any rust. Use a wire brush, if necessary to loosen embedded rust. \u2752 Wipe off all traces of rust. Lightly coat with WD \u2013 40\u00AE to lubricate and provide antirust protection. \u2752 Inspect the push rod and push rod retainer spring. Inspect the pressure plate for looseness and the push rod for wear resulting from misalignment. \u2752 Remove rust with a rust remover and inhibitor such as WD \u2013 40\u00AE. Use a wire brush, if necessary to loosen embedded rust. \u2752 Wipe off all traces of rust. Lightly coat with WD \u2013 40\u00AE to lubricate and provide antirust protection. \u2752 Install a new rubber dust boot over the push rod and piston stem end to protect from moisture and dust.** 2.18.2. Front End Alignment\*\* Perform Major Alignment. Refer to Section 3 of this manual for procedure.![][image\_eZWKKwmUasmO6Ft/UHJV9g==] \r\n\u003C/page\_102\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_103\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* PM - 35\r\n200,000 Miles (321,000 km) Preventive Maintenance 2.19. 200,000 Miles (321,000 km) Preventive Maintenance 2.19.1. Muffler Particulate Filter\*\* Every 200,000 miles or 6,500 operating hours remove the muffler particulate filter and send it to an authorized Cummins repair location for cleaning or replacement. Specialized cleaning equipment is required to perform this maintenance. Refer to Section 4 of this manual for further information on this equipment and the removal procedure for the muffler and the particulate filter.\*\* 2.20. Six Months Preventive Maintenance 2.20.1. PR-2 Pressure Protection Valve\*\* Perform an operating test and leakage check of the valve at this interval. Refer to Section 8 of this manual for procedure.\r\n\u003C/page\_103\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_476\u003E\r\n \r\n\u003C/page\_476\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_477\u003E\r\n**Rev. Retainer, Spring 19. Spring, Graduating 20. O-Ring 21. Locknut, #10 22. Guide, Spring\r\n23. Spring, Stem 24. Stem 25. Piston, Primary 26. Spring, Piston Return 27. Piston, Relay 28. Screw** s023903a.wmf\*\*![][image\_Ueos2HeBfX5epc\u002B2RyPPyQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_477\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_478\u003E\r\n**8 - 48** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nBENDIX E-10 DUAL BRAKE VALVE 5.3.5. Cleaning \u0026 Inspection\*\* 1. Wash all metal parts in mineral spirits and dry. 2. Inspect all parts for excessive wear or deterioration. 3. Inspect the valve seats for nicks or burrs. 4. Check the springs for cracks or corrosion. 5. Replace all rubber parts and any part not found to be serviceable during inspection, using only quality replacement parts.\*\* 5.3.6. Assembly CAUTION: ALWAYS wear safety goggles when performing valve assembly.\*\* Prior to reassembling, lubricate all O-rings, O-ring grooves, piston bores and metal moving surfaces with pneumatic grease. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ All torques specified are assembly torques and can be expected to fall off, after assembly is accomplished. DO NOT retorque after initial assembly torques fall off.\_ 1. Install the No. 1 inlet and exhaust assembly in the upper body and install the retaining ring to secure it. Be sure the retaining ring is seated completely in its groove. 2. Install the large and small O-rings on the relay piston. 3. Install the primary piston O-rings in the piston O-ring groove. 4. Place relay piston spring in concave portion of relay piston and install relay piston through No. 1 inlet/exhaust assembly and into underside of upper body.\r\n5. Place screwdriver, blade up, in vise. Place stem in relay piston - upper body subassembly over the blade of the screwdriver with blade engaged in the slot in the head of the stem. 6. Place the washer over the stem. This washer should be installed in all valves. 7. Install primary piston return spring in upper body piston bore. 8. Install the primary piston. 9. Compress the primary and relay pistons into the upper body from either side and hold them compressed, either manually or mechanically.** CAUTION: Before proceeding with the assembly, note that the lock nut and stem are used to contain the No. 1 piston return spring, stem spring and the relay piston spring. The combined force of these springs is approximately 50 lbs. The primary piston and relay piston should be manually or mechanically contained while the nut and stem are being installed.\*\* 10.Place the stem spring over the spring seat nut and the spring seat over the stem. 11.Install the lock nut on the stem and torque to 20 to 30 in-lb. 12.Install primary piston retaining spring and spring seat, and while depressing spring seat, install retaining snap ring, ensuring snap ring is fully seated in snap ring groove.![][image\_OIrwFJJuCS7ZrhI60jZfoQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_478\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_479\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\r\n\u003C/page\_479\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_585\u003E\r\n\*\* s101902a.wmf\*\*![][image\_B33bxG5Z/UNdyhX8/jJypw==] \r\n\u003C/page\_585\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_586\u003E\r\n**9 - 64** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nMaintenance 11.2. Maintenance WARNING: 1. To prevent serious personal injury, put blocks under the front tires to stop the vehicle from moving. Apply the parking brakes. 2. DO NOT work under a vehicle supported only by jacks. Jacks can slip or fall over and cause serious personal injury. 3. To prevent serious eye injury, ALWAYS wear safe eye protection when performing maintenance or service. 4. Ensure the air system is depressurized before beginning maintenance.\*\* \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ The wheel speed sensors and pulse generating wheels are axle mounted components. Refer to Sections 1 and 2 of this manual for removal and installation procedures for these components.\_** 11.3. Blink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting 11.3.1. Description\*\* ABS diagnostics and troubleshooting can be performed using Blink Code diagnostics or the Pro-Link\u00AE 9000 Diagnostic tool. The ABS Fail indicator on the instrument panel, indicates system status and displays blink code diagnostics. The indicator lamp works as follows: \u2752 When the Master Run switch is set to the DAY-RUN position, the indicator illuminates momentarily for a check and then extinguishes. This indicates the system is O.K. \u2752 The indicator will flash if the normal ABS function is being modified due to road conditions while the vehicle is in operation.\r\n\u2752 The indicator will remain illuminated after the vehicle is started if an existing fault is present. \u2752 After the system is serviced, the indicator will remain illuminated until the vehicle is driven at speeds above 4 mph (6 kp/h). The Blink Code system requires use and interpretation of a series of blink codes. A blink code is a series of flashes emitted by the ABS Fail indicator which describes a system fault or condition. Each blink code contains two digits or sets of flashes: a 1 1/2 second pause occurs between each set of flashes in the digit. A four second pause separates the individual codes displayed. Blink code diagnostics are activated by the ABS switch in the front destination sign compartment. Interpretation of blink codes and further information on troubleshooting and diagnostic procedures is provided in Tables and Charts in this section.\*\* 11.3.2. Diagnostic Mode\*\* Enter diagnostic mode by activating the ABS switch for one second, and then releasing it.\*\* 11.3.3. Clear Mode\*\* Enter clear mode to erase faults from the ECU. This is done by activating the ABS switch for three seconds and then releasing it. If the system displays eight quick flashes followed by a system configuration code, the clear was successful. If this does not occur, faults are still active and must be repaired before they can be cleared.\*\* 11.3.4. System Configuration Code\*\* This is a one digit code consisting of two flashes repeated every four seconds by the ABS switch during clear mode to indicate that all ABS faults have been cleared from ECU memory.![][image\_TAsTlxJ2lWMdKauz1Iu0UA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_586\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_587\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 9 - 65\r\nBlink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting 11.3.5. J1922 or J1939 Datalink Verification **The Pro-Link\u00AE diagnostic tool provides the easiest way to verify the datalink connection, however, the ABS switch can also be used to verify this connection. 1. Set the Master Run switch to the DAY-RUN position. 2. Activate the ABS switch and hold for 3 seconds. 3. The ATC indicator (if so equipped) will illuminate and remain illuminated for the entire test. 4. The ABS indicator will illuminate and blink 8 times. 5. The eight quick flashes will be followed by the system configuration code which will be repeated during the entire test. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ If the ABS lamp does not blink 8 times, there are faults in the system that must be cleared before continuing the test.\_ 6. Activate the J1922 or J1939 engine torque reduction code as follows:\r\na. Press on the accelerator to increase engine speed to 1,000 RPM. b. Keep pressure on the accelerator to maintain throttle position during the entire test. c. With the engine running at 1,000 RPM, activate the ABS switch for 1 second and release for 1 second, then reactivate for 1 second and release. d. The engine will go to idle speed for approximately 10 seconds and then return to 1,000 RPM. This will verify the engine torque reduction command. e. Set the Master Run switch to the STOPENGINE position.** 11.3.6. Toolbox Software\*\*\r\n\u003C/page\_587\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_37\u003E\r\n**System Description** The vehicle\u2019s destination/route signs are controlled by an Operator\u2019s Display Keyboard (ODK) located in the panel of the front destination sign access door. The ODK functions to control and verify the destination/route sign message display. Destination sign message codes are entered into the system data processor using the keypad switches on the front panel of the ODK. The codes translate into message writing data preprogrammed into the system\u2019s memory. The message writing data then controls the signs to display the selected information. The system data processor begins sending and updating message writing data for the ODK to display when the system is powered-up. Turning the Master Run switch from STOP-ENGINE to DAY-RUN or NIGHT-RUN will power-up the system. Boot and application code versions momentarily display when power is applied to the ODK, followed by a brief system initialization message. The last message entered before power shutdown then displays on the ODK. Powering-down occurs when the Master Run switch is turned to STOP-ENGINE. Upon powering-down, front and side destination signs will blank immediately or after a preset delay.**Operating the ODK** Basic operation of the Sign System involves presetting transit authority message codes into the sign system using the ODK. The message codes correlate to preprogrammed destination names, public relations messages, and route numbers unique to each transit authority. If required, multiple sets of message codes may be entered to allow for a quick and complete sign change while in route. Key function and code entry instructions are described in the two sections that follow.![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_37\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_38\u003E\r\n**32** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREAODK Keypad Switches\*\* The ODK contains 28 keypad switches. Certain keypad switches, or groups, may not function if they are not needed for coded entry procedures. Switches that are not enabled either have no effect when pressed, or indicate that particular function is not available. Keypad switch functions are as follows:\*\* \u007F\*\* P/R - press to enable public relations message code entry.\*\* \u007F\*\* ROUTE - press to enable route number entry. Route number entry may be either coded or be the actual route number for display.\*\* \u007F\*\* DEST A, DEST B - press the appropriate key (A or B) to enable destination message code entry and/or message display change.\*\* \u007F\*\* 0-9 Group - key switches are for sequential entering of message codes. These keys function only after a destination (DEST A, DEST B) or enabled public relations (P/R) switch is pressed.\*\* \u007F\*\* A-F Group - key switches are for sequential entering of message codes that contain letters and numbers. Normally these keys will only enable if they are part of an existing message code.\*\* \u007F\*\* ENTER - press to activate the selected message during code entry and during operation.\*\* \u007F\*\* MENU - press to access advanced programming options. Consult your transit authority before use.\*\* \u007F\*\* SELECT - press to select additional characters G-Z when entering message code(s).\*\* \u007F\*\* MESSAGE TEXT - key switch is not currently in use. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Code entry sequences must be followed to set-up destination sign messages. A \u201Cbeep\u201D sounds and a visual read-out appears on the ODK display when pressing any enabled keypad switch.\_** Code Entry\*\* When powering-up the sign system it will display the messages entered previously. If continuing on the same route, re-entering new codes may not be required. To enter a new set of message codes: 1. Consult the transit authority code list for the code that corresponds to your route. 2. Press the switch DEST A, DEST B, P/R, or ROUTE for the message code you are about to enter.![][image\_/X6/ZhnlraOga6tcoZZVtg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_38\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_39\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 33**3. For each code press the corresponding switches in the 0 though 9 and/or A through F groupings one at a time and in proper sequence. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ If the message code contains letter(s) G-Z, press the SELECT key for access.\_ 4. Press the ENTER key switch and allow the ODK to display the actual message. 5. Repeat steps 1 to 4 for each code required for the route. To change from one preset message to another for a turn-around or while in route press the appropriate DEST A or DEST B switch.Figure 12: Operator\u2019s Display Keyboard (ODK)0 ENTER DEST A DEST B P/R MENU A B CDEF\r\nMESSAGE DISPLAY ROUTE SWITCH DESTINATION A \u0026 B SWITCHES \u00220\u0022 THROUGH \u00229\u0022 NUMBER SWITCHES\r\nMENU SWITCH \u0022A\u0022 THROUGH \u0022F\u0022 SWITCHES P/R (PUBLIC RELATIONS) SWITCH ENTER SWITCH\r\nROUTE\r\n23 4 5 6 789 SELECT MESSAGE TEXT Luminator\r\nFLASH CARD PROGRAMMING PORT\r\n\u003C/page\_39\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_396\u003E\r\n Rev. Split Flange Torque Specifications \u261E** NOTE:**\_ Perform torque procedure in two steps using a cross-pattern.\_7/8\u0022 -14 1 3/16 - 12 83 to 90 113 to 122 1\u0022 -16 1 5/16 - 12 112 to 123 152 to 166 1 1/4\u0022 -20 1 5/8 - 12 146 to 161 198 to 218 1 1/2\u0022 -24 1 7/8 - 12 154 to 170 209 to 230 2\u0022 -32 2 1/2 - 12 218 to 240 296 to 325**SAE J514 STRAIGHT THREAD O-RING BOSS (ORB) TORQUE\r\nINCH SIZE DASH SIZE THREAD SIZE (IN.) SWIVEL NUT TORQUE ft-lb. Nm\r\n SAE J518 CODE 61 SPLIT FLANGE TORQUE SPLIT FLANGE DASH SIZE INITIAL TORQUE FINAL TORQUE\*\* -08 8.5 ft-lb. (12 Nm) 15 to 19 ft-lb. (20 to 26 Nm) -12 13 ft-lb. (18 Nm) 21 to 29 ft-lb. (28 to 39 Nm) -16 15 ft-lb. (20 Nm) 27 to 35 ft-lb. (37 to 47 Nm) -20 20 ft-lb. (27 Nm) 35 to 46 ft-lb. (47 to 62 Nm) -24 26 ft-lb. (35 Nm) 46 to 58 ft-lb. (62 to 79 Nm) -32 30 ft-lb. (41 Nm) 54 to 67 ft-lb. (73 to 91 Nm)![][image\_4jeyA2m7Y\u002Bpe5mEgBwLOpA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_396\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_397\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 6 - 33\r\nHYDRAULIC HOSES 2.10.6. Installation\*\* 1. A hose may change in length under pressure. Always provide some slack in the hose to allow for this shortening or elongation. However, excessive slack in the hose may cause poor appearance. See \u201CFig. 6-15: Hose Slack\u201D on page 34. 2. If a hose is installed with a twist in it, operating pressures tend to force it straight. This can loosen the fitting nut. Twisting can cause reinforcement separation and the hose could burst at the point of strain. See \u201CFig. 6-16: Hose Twisting\u201D on page 34. 3. Hose lines that pass near an exhaust manifold or other heat source should be insulated by a heat-resistant boot, firesleeve, or a metal baffle. In any application, brackets and clamps keep hoses in place and reduce abrasion. For installations where abrasion to hose cover cannot be prevented with the use of clamps or brackets, a steel protective coil or abrasion resistant sleeve should be placed over the hose. See \u201CFig. 6-17: Hose Routing\u201D on page 34. **CAUTION: Carefully distribute hose slack between the hose connections and the clamps when securing the line support clamps. DO NOT bend or force the hose to a sharp angle at any location. Use gradual curves for all routing.\r\nNEVER pinch the hose line or subject the hose to tension, torsion, compression or shear stress when installing clamps. DO NOT use undersize or oversize hose support clamps when installing hoses.** 4. At bends, provide sufficient hose so that it does not have a bend radius less than its recommended minimum bend radius. Too tight a bend may kink the hose and restrict or stop the fluid flow. In many cases, the proper use of adapters and hose fittings can eliminate tight bends or kinks. See \u201CFig. 6-18: Hose Bend Radius\u201D on page 34. 5. In applications where there is considerable vibration or flexing, allow additional hose length. The metal hose fittings, of course, are not flexible, and proper installation protects metal parts from undue stress, and avoids kinks in the hose. See \u201CFig. 6-19: Hose Length\u201D on page 35. 6. The use of 90\u00B0 adapters makes hose assemblies neater looking and easier to inspect and maintain. See \u201CFig. 6-20: Hose Adapters\u201D on page 35. 7. Ensure the installed hose assembly is positioned so that the flexible portion of the hose extends at least 1 1/2 times the hose diameter before the hose bends. 8. Rotate and clamp the hose to prevent it from contacting another hose line or abrasion point. 9. Tighten all hose and fittings. Refer to \u201CTorque Specifications\u201D in this section for procedure.\r\n\u003C/page\_397\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_397\u003E\r\n![][image\_WIAtXtMFQj6uPqKnFqIjMQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_397\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_398\u003E\r\n**6 - 34** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nHYDRAULIC HOSES**Fig. 6-15: Hose Slack\r\nFig. 6-16: Hose Twisting\r\nFig. 6-17: Hose Routing\r\nFig. 6-18: Hose Bend Radius\r\nWRONG\r\nRIGHT**s019802a.wmf**WRONG RIGHT**s019803a.wmf**WRONG\r\nRIGHT**s019804a.wmf**RIGHT WRONG\r\n1\u0022\r\nMINIMUM BEND RADIUS 90 o**s019805a.wmf\*\*![][image\_TcQhyw6huP4bOsKXeLIQvQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_398\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_399\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 6 - 35\r\nHYDRAULIC HOSES**Fig. 6-19: Hose Length\r\nFig. 6-20: Hose Adapters**2.10.7. Hose Replacement CAUTION: The following replacement procedure is intended for use on hoses designed with reusable fittings. DO NOT use this procedure on hoses designed with crimped style fittings.\*\* 1. Cut hose square with fine-tooth hacksaw or cut-off wheel. Clean hose bore. See \u201CFig. 6-21: Hose Cutting\u201D on page 36. 2. Put socket in vise. Screw hose counterclockwise into socket until it bottoms. Back off 1/4 turn. Back off hose 1/4 to 1/2 turn. See \u201CFig. 6-22: Hose Insertion into Socket\u201D on page 36. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ When assembling long lengths of hose, put hose in vise just tight enough to prevent from turning, and screw socket onto the hose counter-clockwise until it bottoms. \_3. Lubricate nipple and inside of hose liberally. Use heavy oil or hose assembly lube. See \u201CFig. 6-23: Hose Lubrication\u201D on page 36. 4. Screw nipple clockwise into socket and hose. Tighten nipple leaving 1/32\u0022 to 1/16\u0022 clearance between nipple hex and socket. For swivel ends, screw nipple clockwise into socket and hose. Leave 1/32\u0022 to 1/16\u0022 clearance between nut and socket. Clean and inspect all assemblies. See \u201CFig. 6-24: Hose Nipple Installation\u201D on page 36. \r\nWRONG\r\nRIGHT**s019806a.wmf**RIGHT WRONG**s019807a.wmf\*\*![][image\_5NosPZU/0OJmau5rk1yF/w==] \r\n\u003C/page\_399\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_400\u003E\r\n**6 - 36** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nHYDRAULIC HOSES**Fig. 6-21: Hose Cutting\r\nFig. 6-22: Hose Insertion into Socket\r\nFig. 6-23: Hose Lubrication\r\nFig. 6-24: Hose Nipple Installation**s021402a.wmf\r\ns021403a.wmf\r\ns021404a.wmf\r\ns021405a.wmf\*\*![][image\_iCrUPCiSy/Pua1HK\u002Bwkv2w==] \r\n\u003C/page\_400\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_401\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 6 - 37\r\nHYDRAULIC HOSES 2.10.8. Cleaning\*\* 1. Clean assembly by blowing out with clean compressed air. See \u201CFig. 6-25: Hose Cleaning\u201D on page 37. 2. Assemblies may be rinsed out with mineral spirits if the tube stock is compatible with oil. Otherwise, use hot water at 180\u00B0F (82\u00B0C) maximum.\r\nFig. 6-25: Hose Cleaning**2.10.9. Inspection** 1. Examine hose assembly internally for cut or bulged tube, obstruction, and cleanliness. Check for proper gap (1/32\u0022 to 1/16\u0022) between hex and socket. Nuts should swivel freely. See \u201CFig. 6-26: Hose Inspection\u201D on page 37. 2. Check the lay line of the hose to be sure that the assembly is not twisted. Cap the ends of the hose with plastic covers to keep clean.\r\nFig. 6-26: Hose Inspection\*\* s020202a.wmf s020203a.wmf\*\*![][image\_cDmqfEyfA7DxRSdilx9iDA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_401\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_402\u003E\r\n**6 - 38** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\r\n\u003C/page\_402\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_73\u003E\r\n\*\* 67Stop Lights\*\* The stop lights indicator illuminates when the rear stop lights are on. If the indicator is not illuminated, check for rear stop light failure.**Low Air** The Low Air indicator illuminates to warn of an unsafe air system pressure level. A warning buzzer sounds when the Low Air indicator is activated. DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE until the alarm system is canceled. The air pressure gauge indicates the air system pressure levels of the air brake system. The air system will maintain pressure levels above the low operating limit of 105 psi (724 kPa) during normal vehicle operation.**Check Engine** The Check Engine indicator on the instrument panel illuminates momentarily before starting. The Check Engine indicator extinguishes before the engine starts. If the Check Engine indicator remains illuminated, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.**Shift Selector Display** At engine start-up the shift selector\u2019s red display shows \\[N\\] to indicate that the transmission is in neutral. This should occur automatically at each engine start-up.**No Gen** When illuminated, the No Gen indicator signals that the alternator is NOT charging. The indicator remains illuminated until the engine starts. If the indicator fails to remain illuminated until the engine starts, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.**Operator Display Keyboard (ODK) Messages** Check that the destination sign control unit correctly programs electronic destination sign messages.![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_73\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_74\u003E\r\n**68** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nVEHICLE OPERATION\r\nRear Door Open Indicator\*\* Move the door controller to position #3, #4 or #5 to check that the Rear Door Open indicator illuminates when the doors open. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Exit doors will open and the interlocks will be engaged.\_ Turning the door controller handle to position #1 closes the entrance and exit doors and extinguishes the Rear Door Open indicator. Check that the exit doors are closed. If the exit doors are not closed and the Rear Door Open indicator is still illuminated, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.Day-Time Operation When the engine is operating, check the following:** \u007F\*\* The air system pressure is between 105 and 125 psi (724 and 862 kPa) and the suspension is at full height. The air system requires a working pressure of 105 to 120 psi (724 to 827 kPa).\*\* \u007F\*\* The No Gen indicator is off when the engine is operating.\*\* \u007F\*\* Transmission Shift Selector neutral \\[N\\] indicator remains illuminated.\*\* \u007F\*\* Parking brake and stop light indicator remain illuminated as long as the parking brake is applied.\*\* \u007F\*\* Daytime running lights operation.\*\* \u007F\*\* Front, side and rear destination/route sign lights.\*\* \u007F\*\* Door controller operation.\*\* \u007F\*\* The Door Master switch, when placed in the OFF position, disables the exit door and inhibits the brake interlocks.\*\* \u007F\*\* Aisle lights operation.\*\* \u007F\*\* Return the Door Master switch to the ON position.\*\* \u007F\*\* Wiper and washer controls operation.\*\* \u007F\*\* Defroster/heater control (on dash) operation.![][image\_/X6/ZhnlraOga6tcoZZVtg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_74\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_75\u003E\r\n**VEHICLE OPERATIONRev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 69Night-Time Operation\*\* For night-time operations, ensure the Master Run switch is placed in the NIGHT-RUN position. Check the following in addition to the day-time checks:\*\* \u007F\*\* Instrument panel illumination lights.\*\* \u007F\*\* Headlight operation (high and low beam).\*\* \u007F\*\* Front and rear identification and marker lights.\*\* \u007F\*\* Tail lights.\*\* \u007F\*\* License plate light.\*\* \u007F\*\* Panel lights dimmer changes the brightness of instrumentation backlights and panel text.\*\* \u007F\*\* Interior fluorescent lights can be turned on using the Aisle Lights switch.**Pre-Trip Brake Test \r\nWARNING: Before driving the vehicle conduct the following test sequence. If the test reveals a fault, advise service personnel and DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.** Conduct the following test sequence to ensure that the air brake system is functioning properly. 1. Apply the parking brake. 2. Start the engine, set the Idle Speed switch to FAST and check the following: a. The low pressure warning devices switch off as the air pressure builds. b. If the air pressure gauge reading was below 90 psi (620 kPa), the reading increases back to 90 psi (620 kPa) in less than three minutes. c. The air pressure gauge reading levels off at 120 to 125 psi (827 to 862 kPa). 3. Release the parking brake. a. Make multiple light brake treadle applications and check the following: i. The air pressure gauge reading stabilizes at 105 psi (724 kPa) as the air compressor begins its pumping cycle. \r\n\u003C/page\_75\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_56\u003E\r\n\*\* op0494a.wmf\*\*![][image\_ClShyE8L5qLPx\u002BZwjDvaJQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_56\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_57\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 51Master Run Switch\*\* The Master Run Switch is a 4-position rotary switch. The DAY-RUN, NIGHT-RUN, and NIGHT PARK positions are used to activate the vehicle Multiplexing System and energize various 12/24V electrical circuits. The STOP-ENGINE position is used to shutdown the engine and de-energize the Multiplexing System and most 12/24V electrical circuits except those associated with safety functions. The Battery Disconnect switch must be set to the OFF position in order to disconnect the remaining 12/24V circuits from the vehicle batteries. The following table provides a list of circuits energized by the various Master Run switch positions: \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ The Multiplexing System is programmed to remain active for 30 minutes after the Master Run Switch is set to the STOP-ENGINE position.\_**MASTER RUN SWITCH OPERATION CIRCUIT OR SYSTEM STOPENGINE DAYRUN NIGHTRUN NIGHTPARK\*\* Daytime running lights x Headlights (high \u0026 low beam) x Four-way hazard lights x x x x Turn lights\\*\\*\\\* x x x x Stop lights x x Clearance/marker lights x x Tail lights xx License plate light x x Backup lights \u0026 alarm x x Aisle lights (normal) x x x Aisle lights (on)\\*\\*\\\* x x x x Instrument panel illumination x x Instrument panel dimmer x x![][image\_aHnbgRwjq3zB6SUiM0yszQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_57\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_58\u003E\r\n**52** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREA**Driver\u2019s lamp\\*****\\*****\\\* x x x x Service compartment lights\\*****\\*****\\\* x x x x Entrance \u0026 exit door lights with door open \\*****\\***** x x x Instrument panel warning indicators x x Transmission shift selector x x Brake \u0026 accelerator interlocks x x Destination sign operation x x x Door controller x x x Horns x x x x Retarder \\\* x x Driver\u2019s alarm x x Parking brake alarm\\*****\\*****\\\* x x Kneeling operation \u0026 alarm x x Wheelchair ramp \u0026 alarm x x x Passenger signal system x x Public address system x x HVAC system \\\* x x Auxiliary heater x x Intermittent wiper control x x Remote mirrors x x Heated mirrors x x \\\* Engine must be running \\*****\\***** DAY-RUN also requires W/C ramp deployed \\*****\\*****\\\*Multiplexing system must be active**MASTER RUN SWITCH OPERATION CIRCUIT OR SYSTEM STOPENGINE DAYRUN NIGHTRUN NIGHTPARK\*\*![][image\_cDZreiIQVcvvTlZG9AKySA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_58\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_59\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 53Door Controller\r\nWARNING: Positioning the Door Master switch to OFF disables the brake interlocks and the exit door controller.\*\* The door controller opens and closes the entrance and exit doors. The five positions of the controller and the related door functions are as follows:\*\* \u007F\*\* Position #1: Entrance door closed, exit doors disabled.\*\* \u007F\*\* Position #2: Entrance door open, exit doors disabled.\*\* \u007F\*\* Position #3: Entrance door open, exit doors enabled.\*\* \u007F\*\* Position #4: Entrance door closed, exit doors enabled.\*\* \u007F\*\* Position #5: Entrance door open, exit doors enabled. When the exit door is open, the brake and accelerator interlocks apply automatically and the stop lights indicator illuminates.**Figure 16: Door Controller**4\r\n2\r\n1\r\n3\r\n5**op0601a.wmf**![][image\_Jv/WQKO0WKguPINghbtKAQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_59\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_60\u003E\r\n**54** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREA\r\nPedal Adjustment Switch\*\* The Pedal Adjustment switch controls the position of the brake and accelerator pedals. The FWD position moves the pedal assembly forward, the RWD position moves the assembly back and the Hold position will maintain the desired adjustment.**Door Master Switch\r\nWARNING: Greater attention to passenger safety must be given whenever operating the vehicle with the Door Master switch in the OFF position, as this position disables several safety features and will allow the following conditions to occur: \u007F Vehicle can be moved with entrance and/or exit door open (brake interlocks disabled). \u007F Transmission can be shifted without foot on brake treadle. \u007F Transmission can be shifted and vehicle moved with wheelchair ramp deployed. \u007F Exit doors can be opened at any speed by using the emergency release control valve.** The Door Master toggle switch controls power to the brake interlocks and exit door. When the switch is in the ON position, the entrance and exit doors are fully functional. In this mode, opening the exit door, kneeling the vehicle or operating the wheelchair ramp engages the interlocks. Engaging the interlocks applies the rear brakes and deactivates the accelerator. In the OFF position, the brake interlocks are released (interlocks will not engage). The entrance door remains fully functional and the exit door does not function. A warning buzzer sounds and the Rear Door Open indicator illuminates on the instrument panel. In this mode, the exit door only opens if the emergency release control valve is activated. The control valve is located behind the breakable window to the left of the exit door.\r\n\u003C/page\_60\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_589\u003E\r\n \r\n\u003C/page\_589\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_590\u003E\r\n**9 - 68** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nBlink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting 11.3.8. Blink Code Conditions\r\n11.3.9. Blink Code Troubleshooting \u0026 Repair BLINK CODE CONDITIONS CONDITION REASON ACTION\*\* ABS Fail indicator does not illuminate at ignition. Defective luminescent strip. Replace luminescent strip. Check connections. Make necessary repairs. Voltage not within acceptable range (18-30 volts for a 24-volt system). Check connections. Measure voltage. Make necessary repairs. Can\u2019t use blink code diagnostics: ABS Fail indicator will not extinguish when blink code is activated. Switch not held for correct time: 1 Second - Diagnostics Mode 3 Seconds - Clear All Mode Repeat procedure, hold switch for correct time. Improper or faulty wiring. Inspect and repair wiring. Eight Flashes not received after blink code switch pressed for at least three seconds, then released. Active Faults still exist. Identify active faults, then make necessary repairs. Turn ignition OFF, then repeat Blink Code Diagnostics.\*\* BLINK CODE TROUBLESHOOTING \u0026 REPAIR BLINK CODE ACTION REQUIRED REFERENCE\*\* 2.1 2.2 2.3 2.4 2.5 2.6 Check ABS modulator valve, cable and connectors. Check valve resistance (11-21 ohms). Valve resistance checks.\r\n3.1 3.2 3.3 3.4 3.5 3.6 Check sensor gap. Adjust wheel sensor to touch tooth wheel. Check for loose wheel bearings or excessive hub runout. Check 0.2 volts AC output @ 30 RPM.\r\nSensor adjustment checks. Section 1 - Front Axle. Section 2 - Rear Axle. Sensor voltage test. 4.1 4.2 4.3 4.4 4.5 4.6 Check sensor, sensor cable and connectors. Check sensor resistance (900-2000 ohms). Sensor resistance checks. 5.1 5.2 5.3 5.4 5.5 5.6 Check for tire size mismatch. Check that each tooth wheel has the same amount of teeth. Check sensor, sensor cable and connector for intermittent contact.\r\nFront \u0026 Rear tire size must be within \u00B1 14% of each other.\r\n6.1 6.2 6.3 6.4 6.5 6.6 Check for damaged tooth wheel.![][image\_z4\u002B42LAQ2rcGGDCb67\u002BzdA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_590\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_591\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 9 - 69\r\nBlink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting**7-1\\\* Check for proper data link connection (J1922 and J1939). Verify wheel spin on each axle. 7.2\\\* Check ATC valve, valve cables and connectors. Check ATC valve resistance (28-49 ohms). Valve resistance checks.\r\n7.3\\\* Check retarder (third brake) connections. Refer to ABS Electrical Schematic. 7.4\\\* Check ABS Fail indicator connections. Verify blink code switch was activated longer than 16 seconds. 7.5\\\* Verify proper ATC set up. 7.6\\\* Verify accuracy of blink code and clear from ECU memory. 8.1 Check for low voltage. Check vehicle voltage, fuse and supply to ECU (18-30 volts). Electrical Schematic \u0026 voltage checks. 8.2 Check for high voltage. Check vehicle voltage (18-30 volts). Verify accuracy of blink code and clear from ECU memory. Voltage checks \u0026 blink code diagnostics.\r\n8.3 Verify accuracy of blink code and clear from ECU memory. Blink code diagnostics \u0026 \u201CClear Mode\u201D. 8.4 Verify all ECU connectors are in place. Verify accuracy of blink code and clear from ECU memory. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ If code does not clear, it may be necessary to replace the ECU.\_Contact Meritor Customer Service Center at 1-800-535-5560.\r\n8.5 Check resistance of ABS ground connections (less than 1 ohm). Refer to ABS Electrical Schematic. \\\* If this code continues after all repairs have been made, or if a code is received for a component that is not installed on the vehicle, it may be necessary to reconfigure the ECU. Contact the Meritor Customer Service Center.** BLINK CODE TROUBLESHOOTING \u0026 REPAIR BLINK CODE ACTION REQUIRED REFERENCE\*\*![][image\_r\u002BSE56xTFA7SUGMWt7C7wg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_591\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_592\u003E\r\n**9 - 70** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\r\n\u003C/page\_592\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_86\u003E\r\n![][image\_/X6/ZhnlraOga6tcoZZVtg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_86\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_87\u003E\r\n**WHEELCHAIR SYSTEMRev. Fasten the belt ends around a solid frame member of the wheelchair. Insert the belt clip into the belt buckle. 5. (204) 982-8431 Fax (204) 667-5769 http\://www\.newflyer.com Printed in Canada![][image\_rvK9qxwxTA2wBKXXSYcG\u002BA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_92\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_67\u003E\r\n** \u007F\*\* Check that the Door Master switch is in the ON position.\*\* \u007F\*\* Check horn operation.**Transmission Operation\r\nCAUTION: Be sure to bring the vehicle to a full stop before shifting from drive \\[D\\] to reverse \\[R\\] or vice versa. Selection of the automatic transmission operating ranges is by the shift selector module on the instrument panel. Six click-in style push button switches illuminate to indicate the range selected and are labeled \\[R\\] for reverse, \\[N\\] for neutral, and \\[D, 3, 2, 1\\] for drive.** Selection of the automatic transmission operating ranges is by the shift selector module on the instrument panel. There are six operating range selection buttons for reverse, neutral and drive \\[labeled R, N, D, 3, 2, 1\\]. The D, 3, 2, 1 buttons allow control of the forward gears. Operate the transmission using the following procedure: 1. Before starting the engine a. Check that the transmission is in neutral \\[N\\]. b. Check that the parking brake is on.![][image\_3N4FoSS9nZHyPXEvEo2b8Q==] \r\n\u003C/page\_67\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_68\u003E\r\n**62** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nVEHICLE OPERATION**c. Apply the brake treadle. 2. With the engine running and idling at normal speed, apply firm pressure on the brake treadle and make the desired range selection. 3. Release parking brake and the brake treadle to proceed. 4. To change direction bring the vehicle to a full stop, apply firm pressure on the brake treadle and make the desired range selection. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ A back-up alarm activates when reverse \\[R\\] is selected.\_ 5. When parking or shutting down the vehicle come to a full stop, apply the parking brake, select neutral \\[N\\] and release the brake treadle. Retarder Operation The retarder is used to slow the vehicle and works in conjunction with the service (air) brakes. The retarder, located inside the transmission, is a fluid brake that creates driveline deceleration. When activated, its housing fills with transmission fluid which impedes output shaft and rotor rotation slowing the vehicle. The retarder operates at speeds above 5 mph and engages with brake treadle application. Lightly pressing on the brake treadle (the first 5\u00B0 to 10\u00B0 of movement) engages the retarder into the first of three progressive stages of operation. Further brake application engages the remaining stages leading to full retarder operation. Releasing the brake treadle or decreasing speed to below 5 mph will disengage the retarder. The retarder can also be disabled using the Retarder Disable switch on the driver\u2019s side console. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ Consult your transit authority for specific operating conditions during which the Retarder Disable switch should be used.\_**Anti-Lock Braking System\*\* The Anti-Lock Braking System (ABS) functions to bring the vehicle to a safe, controlled stop during emergency braking situations. Through computer monitoring of wheel speeds the system controls brake pressure to prevent wheel lock-up. If during brake application the ABS system senses imminent wheel lock-up it engages automatically thus increasing vehicle stability and control. The ABS is inactive (no ABS event) whenever wheel deceleration difference remains within programmed limits.![][image\_/X6/ZhnlraOga6tcoZZVtg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_68\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_69\u003E\r\n**VEHICLE OPERATIONRev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 63**An ABS Indicator on the instrument panel indicates any active faults and is also used by service personnel to retrieve codes. WARNING: Keep stopping distances the same as those for similar nonABS equipped vehicles. To operate under normal conditions use the standard braking technique. For emergency braking apply firm and constant pressure to the brake treadle. If required the ABS system will activate automatically producing a pulsing sensation to the brake treadle and a hissing sound. These are normal indications of ABS system operation. During emergency braking avoid \u201Cpumping\u201D the brakes as this defeats the pulsing action of the ABS system and will increase your stopping distance. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ Under certain operating conditions, the ABS system will override the transmission retarder. Refer to \u201CRetarder Operation\u201D in this manual for specific operating conditions which apply.\_ If the ABS on one wheel malfunctions the system will retain normal braking on that wheel. Should the entire ABS System malfunction the system will also retain normal braking. The ABS Fail indicator on the instrument panel will illuminate if a malfunction occurs. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ After ABS System service the ABS Fail indicator will remain illuminated at engine start-up. Driving the vehicle above 4 mph should extinguish the indicator. If the indicator remains illuminated, active faults are still present; contact service personnel.\_\r\n\u003C/page\_69\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_638\u003E\r\n** 2.11.4. Troubleshooting\*\* Troubleshooting is normally limited to the isolation of defective components. This is done through the interpretation of fault codes. A fault code will be generated in the event of an operational failure. The code event is only visible during the after run (cool down) period of operation.\*\* 2.11.4.1.Storing a Fault Code in Memory\*\* Once the Thermo 230/300 completes the after run (cool down) period initiated by a failure event, the current code will be downloaded (stored) in memory.\r\nThe Thermo 230/300 can store up to ten fault codes. Once the memory is \u201Cfull\u201D, any additional fault code will replace the earliest code stored thereby continually updating the fault codes stored in memory with the most recent faults.\*\* 2.11.4.2.Reading \u0026 Removing Fault Codes Stored in Memory\*\* An IBM compatible computer with the necessary software can be used to read and remove (reset) stored fault codes from the Thermo 230/300 memory. This is achieved through the use of a diagnostic kit connected to the Thermo 230/ 300 In addition to working with stored fault codes, the PC Download Diagnostic Kit allows you to do several other functions such as reading values while the heater is in operation or testing individual components. Printing out of fault codes is also available (User supplied printer required). The PC Download Diagnostic Kit comes with software, an adapter harness, and instructions, and can be obtained from New Flyer Parts. Refer to Section 10 of your New Flyer Parts Manual for the ordering of kits.\*\* 2.11.4.3.PC System Requirements\*\* \u2752 IBM compatible PC with 80286 processor or higher \u2752 DOS version 3.0 or higher including at least 1MB RAM \u2752 Hard disk with at least 3 MB space available \u2752 3 1/2 inch, 1.44 MB floppy disk drive for installation of program files \u2752 VGA graphics board with 640 x 480 pixel resolution and at least 16 colors \u2752 Unused serial port \u2752 Monitor, keyboard (mouse or other pointing device recommended)![][image\_CZzC7ZAD\u002BNDZoG/TTiLQgA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_638\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_639\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 10 - 37\r\nAUXILIARY COOLANT HEATER 2.11.4.4.Thermo 230/300 Fault Codes\*\* The following table lists the possible faults that can be read by using the PC Download Diagnostic kit.\*\* THERMO 230/300 FAULT CODES FAILURE SYMPTOM CAUSE CHECK \u0026 CORRECT 1X Flash (F 01)\*\* No combustion after completion of start up sequence. Fuel system Fuel level Type of fuel being used Fuel filter Fuel line connections (air bubbles in fuel lines) Fuel nozzle plugged Combustion air Air intake or exhaust, restricted or plugged Electronic ignition Incorrect electrode gap\*\* 2X Flashes (F 02)\*\* Flame out during burner operation. No restart possible. Fuel supply (shortage of fuel) Restriction in the fuel system Fuel filter Fuel line connections (air bubbles in fuel lines) Type of fuel being used\*\* 3X Flashes (F 03)\*\* Low voltage for more than 20 seconds. Electrical system Load test batteries Corrosion at connections Loose connections\*\* 4X Flashes (F 04)\*\* Flame detector recongnizes false flame signal during prestart or shut-down cycle. Defective flame detector Replace flame detector**5X Flashes (F 05)** Flame detector. Wiring Damaged wiring, open or short circuit Defective flame detector Replace flame detector\*\* 6X Flashes (F 06)\*\* Temperature sensor. Wiring Damaged wiring, open or short circuit Defective temperature sensor Replace temperature detector![][image\_vZRvMq3zE408Dx6ob5la4g==] \r\n\u003C/page\_639\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_640\u003E\r\n**10 - 38** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nAUXILIARY COOLANT HEATER7X Flashes (F 07)\*\* Fuel solenoid valve. Wiring Damaged or corroded wiring, open or shor circuit Defective solenoid valve Replace solenoid valve\*\* 8X Flashes (F 08)\*\* Combustion air fan motor. Wiring Damaged wiring, open or short circuit Wrong RPM Replace combustion air fan Defective combustion air fan motor Replace\*\* 9X Flashes (F 09)\*\* Circulation pump motor. Wiring Damaged wiring, open or short circuit Defective circulation pump motor Replace circulation pump motor\*\* 10X Flashes (F 10)\*\* Temperature limiter. Overheat condition Reset temperature limiter Coolant flow Coolant level or flow restriction Air trapped in coolant circuit Wiring Damaged or corroded wiring, open or short circuit Defective temperature limiter Replace temperature limiter\r\n\u003C/page\_640\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_69\u003E\r\n**Automatic Traction Control** The vehicle\u2019s Automatic Traction Control (ATC) System activates automatically to prevent drive wheel spin when accelerating or starting the vehicle from a stand still. The system uses components of the ABS System to apply the brakes to a drive wheel that loses traction and spins. This transfers the engine torque to the wheel with better traction. If both drive wheels spin, the system reduces engine torque to improve traction. The ATC indicator on the instrument panel illuminates to confirm system operation.![][image\_sEmJb7STcpg5I\u002BGnZIMcZA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_69\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_70\u003E\r\n**64** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nVEHICLE OPERATIONStarting the Engine\r\nWARNING: Put the shift selector in neutral \\[N\\] and apply the parking brake before starting the engine. If the parking brake indicator does not illuminate, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.\*\* To operate the vehicle the Battery Disconnect switches must be in the ON position. Check the switches by opening the battery disconnect access door at the rear of the vehicle. These connect the engine starter and vehicle electrical circuits to the battery power. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Refer to the Driver\u2019s Check List Section of this manual before operating the vehicle.\_**Figure 18: Battery Disconnect Switch op3437a.wmf\*\*![][image\_jF8ojUVQ6gCcAnrZdvSqMg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_70\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_71\u003E\r\n**VEHICLE OPERATIONRev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 65Master Run Switch\*\* Turn the Master Run switch (on side console) to DAY-RUN or NIGHT-RUN position. This activates the vehicle\u2019s Multiplexing System. Illuminated indicator lights and sounding alarms signify an active Multiplexing System. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ When restarting less than 30 minutes after engine shut down, the Multiplexing System responds instantly.\_**Start Push Button\r\nWARNING: Put the shift selector in neutral \\[N\\] and apply the parking brake before starting the engine. If the parking brake indicator does not illuminate, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.\*\* With the vehicle\u2019s Multiplexing System active, push the Start push button until the engine starter engages and starts the engine. When the engine starts, release the push button. If the starter fails to operate, check the following:\*\* \u007F\*\* The Master Run switch is in the DAY-RUN or NIGHT-RUN position.\*\* \u007F\*\* The Transmission Selector indicator shows neutral \\[N\\].\*\* \u007F\*\* The engine compartment Engine Run switch is in the FRONT position.\*\* \u007F\*\* The parking brake is applied. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ The Multiplexing System limits continuous starter operation to 14 seconds; the starter circuit is then disconnected for 60 seconds to allow the starter to cool down.\_![][image\_PEdoTuxgwuskxnhE5BCckg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_71\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_72\u003E\r\n**66\*\* Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nVEHICLE OPERATIONOperational Checks\*\* Once the engine is operating the operator should observe the following:\*\* \u007F\*\* The air system pressure is between 105 and 125 psi (724 and 862 kPa) and the suspension is at full height. The Air System requires a working pressure of 105 to 120 psi (724 to 827 kPa).\*\* \u007F\*\* The No Gen indicator is off when the engine is operating.\*\* \u007F\*\* Transmission Selector neutral \\[N\\] indicator remains illuminated.\*\* \u007F\*\* Parking brake and stop light indicator remain illuminated as long as the parking brake is applied.\*\* \u007F\*\* Door controller is operational.\*\* \u007F\*\* Position the Door Master switch to the OFF position and attempt to open the exit door by using the side console door controller. The exit door should not be operational; the entrance door should remain operational.\*\* \u007F\*\* Return the Door Master switch to the ON position.\*\* \u007F\*\* Wiper and washer controls are operational.\*\* \u007F\*\* Defroster/heater controls (on dash) are operational.\*\* \u007F\*\* Exterior lights operate during exterior light test. To conduct test, ensure engine is running and parking brake is applied, then press both turn switches simultaneously. All exterior lights will illuminate for two minutes.\*\* \u007F\*\* The destination sign controller is active.**Parking Brake** The parking brake indicator illuminates when the parking brake is applied. If the parking brake indicator is not illuminated, apply the parking brake by pulling up on the parking brake control valve knob. If the parking brake indicator does not illuminate, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE. Press the brake treadle before releasing the parking brake. Release the parking brake by pushing down on the control knob. The parking brake indicator extinguishes. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Reapply parking brake.*****![][image*****/X6/ZhnlraOga6tcoZZVtg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_72\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_73\u003E\r\n**VEHICLE OPERATIONRev. A - 28/03/2008\*\* Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\r\n\u003C/page\_73\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_587\u003E\r\n Toolbox software is a PC based diagnostics program that can display wheel speed data, test individual components, verify installation wiring and more. It runs in Windows\u00AE 95, 98, and NT. An RS 232 to J1708 converter box is required. Versions 3.2 and higher support E version ABS. Refer to user\u2019s manual for complete toolbox software instuctions.![][image\_PpYXCn4pc2t7S28\u002BLibpyA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_587\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_588\u003E\r\n**9 - 66** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 16/05/2008\r\nBlink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting\r\n BLINK CODE DIAGNOSTICS \u0026 TROUBLESHOOTING MODE PROCEDURE SYSTEM RESPONSE ACTION STEP I\*\* Turn ignition ON. Possible responses: 1. ABS Fail indicator illuminates momentarily then extinguishes, indicating System OK.\r\nNo recognizable active faults in the ABS. No action required.\r\n2. ABS Fail indicator does not illuminate, indicating possible wiring fault. Inspect wiring. Make necessary repairs. 3. ABS Fail indicator remains illuminated, indicating: a. Fault, or faults, in the system. Continue with blink code diagnostics. (Go to Step II) b. Sensor fault during last operation. Continue with blink code diagnostics. (Go to Step II) c. Faults cleared from ECU, but vehicle not driven. Drive vehicle - lamp will go out when vehicle reaches 4 mph (6 km/h). DIAGNOSTIC d. ECU disconnected. Connect ECU.\*\* STEP II\*\* Press and hold ABS switch for one second, then release. ABS Fail indicator begins flashing two-digit blink code(s). Determine if fault is active or stored: Active Fault: Indicator will repeatedly display one code. Stored Fault: Indicator will display code for each stored fault then stop blinking. Faults will be displayed one time only.\*\* STEP III\*\* Count the flashes to determine the blink code. First Digit: 1 - 8 flashes, Pause (1 1/2 seconds). Second Digit: 1 - 6 flashes, Pause (4 seconds). Find definition for blink code on Blink Code Identification chart.\*\* STEP IV\*\* Turn ignition OFF. Repair and Record faults. Active Fault. Make necessary repairs. Repeat steps I, II and III until System OK code (1 - 1) received. Stored Faults. Record for future reference. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ Last fault stored is first fault displayed.\_CLEAR**STEP V\*\* Turn Ignition ON. Clear faults from memory: Press and hold blink code switch for at least three seconds, then release.\r\nABS Fail indicator flashes eight times. Eight flashes not received.\r\nAll faults successfully cleared. Turn ignition OFF. Active faults still exist, repeat Steps I through V.![][image\_b1xlxOJG0CeUXTOnR367\u002Bw==] \r\n\u003C/page\_588\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_589\u003E\r\n**Rev. A - 16/05/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 9 - 67\r\nBlink Code Diagnostics \u0026 Troubleshooting 11.3.7. Blink Code Identification\*\* System malfunctions are stored in the ECU as fault codes. A code may be active or stored. Active faults are current faults that must be repaired before they can be cleared from memory, and before additional faults can be displayed. Stored faults can be either repaired active faults that have not been cleared from ECU memory, or intermittent faults which are not currently active.\*\* BLINK CODE IDENTIFICATION First Digit TYPE OF FAULT Second Digit SPECIFIC LOCATION OF FAULT\*\* 1 No Faults\*\* 1\*\* No Faults 2 ABS Modulator Valve\*\* 1\*\* Right Front Steer Axle (curbside) 3 Too much Sensor Gap\*\* 2\*\* Left Front Steer Axle (driver\u2019s side) 4 Sensor Short or Open\*\* 3\*\* Right Rear Drive Axle (curbside) 5 Sensor Signal Erratic\*\* 4\*\* Left Rear Drive Axle (driver\u2019s side) 6 Tooth Wheel\*\* 5\*\* Right Rear/Additional Axle (curbside)**\\\* 6** Left Rear/Additional Axle (driver\u2019s side)**\\\* 1** J1922 or J1939 Datalink\*\* 2\*\* ATC Val ve 7 System Function\\*\\*\*\* 3\*\* Retarder Relay (third brake)\*\* 4\*\* ABS Fail Indicator\*\* 5\*\* ATC Configuration\*\* 6\*\* Reserved for Future Use\*\* 1\*\* Low Power Supply\*\* 2\*\* High Power Supply 8ECU**3** Internal Fault\*\* 4\*\* System Configuration Error\*\* 5\*\* Ground\*\* \\\* \*\* Tandem, lift, tag or pusher axle depending upon the type of suspension.\*\* \\*\\*\*\* If this code continues after all repairs have been made, or if you receive a code for a component that is not installed on the vehicle, it may be necessary to reconfigure the ECU. Contact Meritor Customer Service Center at 1-800-535-5560 for assistance.![][image\_vUdlo\u002BuXp2rsTlQUg1ABlg==]\r\n\u003C/page\_589\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259serv.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259serv.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_42\u003E\r\n\*\* op0395a.wmf\*\*![][image\_1UhXj0QV6ywuR4Su4o1\u002BbQ==] \r\n\u003C/page\_42\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_43\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 37Engine Fire Indicator (Red)\r\nWARNING: If the Engine Fire indicator illuminates, immediately remove the vehicle from traffic to a safe location. Unload the passengers and shut down the vehicle. Contact transit dispatch for assistance.\*\* The Engine Fire indicator illuminates and an alarm sounds if there is a fire in the engine compartment.**Engine Fan Fault (Amber)** The Engine Fan Fault indicator will illuminate if a fault is detected with the fan drive cooling system. A low hydraulic fluid level signal or a high-speed fan with engine cold condition will register as a fault. Notify maintenance personnel if this indicator illuminates.**Exhaust Regen Needed Indicator (Amber)** This indicator illuminates when the engine ECU receives a thermal signal from the muffler sensor indicating that the muffler filter requires a regenerative cycle. Notify service personnel when this occurs.**High Exhaust Temp Indicator (Amber)\r\nWARNING: If the High Exhaust Temp indicator on the instrument panel illuminates, ensure the exhaust outlet is not located where it could cause damage to persons or any materials which could melt or explode, and that nothing is within 2 feet of the outlet. Ensure no combustible materials are within 5 feet of the outlet. Exhaust outlet temperatures can reach 1500\u00B0F (800\u00B0C) when this indicator illuminates.** The High Exhaust Temp indicator illuminates when the muffler thermocouple detects a temperature higher than normal operating conditions. Notify service personnel when this occurs.![][image\_Ul7yo9exlDUkELQo\u002Bcyglg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_43\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_44\u003E\r\n**38** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREA\r\nLow Trans Indicator (Red)\r\nCAUTION: DO NOT drive the vehicle for extended periods with the Low Trans indicator illuminated. Poor transmission performance and eventual transmission damage will result.\*\* The Low Trans indicator illuminates if the oil level within the transmission reaches a level too low for proper transmission operation. Advise service personnel if this indicator illuminates.**Check Trans Indicator (Red)** The Check Trans indicator illuminates if the transmission electronics has detected a potentially serious problem in the transmission. If this indicator illuminates, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.**Check Engine Indicator (Amber)\r\nCAUTION: If after engine start-up the Check Engine indicator remains illuminated, advise service personnel. Avoid extended periods of operation with this indicator illuminated.** The Check Engine indicator illuminates if the engine requires service. The indicator is controlled by the vehicle\u2019s Multiplexing System which monitors engine sensor output. The electronic control system will illuminate the indicator if sensor output signals fall outside of a predetermined range. **Stop Engine Indicator (Red)** The Stop Engine indicator illuminates if an engine operating condition occurs that will result in damage to the engine. The indicator is controlled by the vehicle\u2019s electronic control system which monitors engine sensor output. If the Multiplexing System illuminates the indicator it also initiates an engine shut-down sequence. As an operation check, the Stop Engine indicator should remain illuminated momentarily when the engine is started.![][image\_s9faFLM2rrHDTwQ8ZLUCCg==] \r\n\u003C/page\_44\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_45\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 39\*\*\u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ If this indicator remains illuminated, the engine will continue running for 30 seconds. Use the time to drive out of traffic to a safe area.\_**High Beam Indicator (Blue)\*\* The high beam indicator, symbolized by a lit headlight, illuminates when the vehicle headlights are in the high beam mode of operation. Pressing the dimmer switch returns the headlights to normal low beam operation.**Rear Door Open Indicator (Red)** The Rear Door Open indicator illuminates when the door controller is turned to position #3, #4 or #5 and the exit door opens.**Kneel Indicator (Amber)** The Kneel indicator illuminates when the front suspension is in the kneeling mode and is lowering the vehicle to the curb. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ The Kneel toggle switch is on the instrument panel.\_**Stop Request Indicator (Red)\*\* The Stop Request indicator illuminates when the passenger signal system has been activated.**W/C Stop Request Indicator (Amber)** The Wheelchair Stop Request indicator illuminates when the wheelchair passenger signal system has been activated.![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_45\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_46\u003E\r\n**40** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREA\r\nABS Fail Indicator (Amber)**\r\n\u003C/page\_46\u003E","ContentLocation":"https\://lehub.sharepoint.com/sites/CA-Suivideseffectifs-Qubec/Shared%20Documents/Greg%20Boulon/Manuels%20de%20service/Manuels/New%20Flyer/SR1259/1259oper.pdf?web=1","KnowledgeName":"Manuels","KnowledgeType":"SharePointSearchSource","Title":"1259oper.pdf"},{"Content":"\u003Cpage\_39\u003E\r\n** op1409a.wmf\*\*![][image\_41GPBw8wynJMBAqrD\u002BLn7g==] \r\n\u003C/page\_39\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_40\u003E\r\n**34** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. A - 28/03/2008\r\nDRIVER\u2019S AREAInstrument Panel\r\nTurn Indicators (Green)\r\nWARNING: If turn signal indicators do not operate as described, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.\*\* The turn indicators, symbolized by directional arrows, flash on either side of the instrument panel when the right-hand or left-hand floor-mounted turn signal switch is pressed. When the Hazard switch is activated, both turn indicators flash together. Failure of these lights to flash normally indicates that the flasher module is not functioning.**No Gen Indicator (Red)\r\nCAUTION: If the no gen indicator remains illuminated while the engine is operating, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.** The no gen indicator, symbolized by a battery, illuminates when the alternator is not charging. The no gen indicator illuminates when the Master Run switch is in the DAYRUN or NIGHT-RUN position and the engine is not operating. The no gen indicator turns off once the engine is operating.**Low Oil Indicator (Red)\r\nCAUTION: If the Low Oil alarm continues and the indicator lamp remains illuminated, DO NOT OPERATE THE VEHICLE.** The Low Oil indicator illuminates if the engine oil pressure is too low for proper engine lubrication. The Low Oil indicator is accompanied by a warning buzzer.![][image\_9OfPMYcueybH8wGgWVOAHw==] \r\n\u003C/page\_40\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_41\u003E\r\n**DRIVER\u2019S AREARev. A - 28/03/2008** Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 35**Before starting the engine, positioning the Master Run switch to DAY-RUN or NIGHTRUN illuminates the Low Oil indicator and sounds its alarm. This occurs momentarily and is a normal electrical system test. \u261E** NOTE:**\_ If this indicator remains illuminated, the Engine Protection System engages to initiate an automatic engine shutdown sequence.\_**Low Coolant Indicator (Amber)\*\* The Low Coolant indicator illuminates if too little coolant is in the engine to maintain normal engine operating temperature. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ If this indicator remains illuminated, the Engine Protection System engages to initiate an automatic engine shutdown sequence.\_**Hot Engine Indicator (Red)\*\* The Hot Engine indicator will illuminate if the engine exceeds its normal operating temperature and overheats. The Hot Engine indicator is accompanied by a warning buzzer. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ If this indicator remains illuminated, the Engine Protection System engages, initiating an automatic engine shutdown sequence.\_**Wait to Start Indicator (Amber)\*\* The Wait to Start indicator illuminates before engine start-up with the Master Run switch in the DAY-RUN or NIGHT-RUN position. The indicator will remain illuminated for up to 45 seconds while the intake air heater system operates. \u261E\*\* NOTE:**\_ The Wait to Start indicator and the intake air heater system will only operate in temperatures below 66\u00B0F (19\u00B0C).\_![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_41\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_42\u003E\r\n**36\*\* Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev. [...] The HEAT position commands the heating system to warm the vehicle interior to a preset temperature. In the VENT position, the system draws fresh air into the vehicle. The COOL position commands the air conditioning system to cool the vehicle interior to a preset temperature. The OFF position deactivates the system. Dash Fan Switches The RH and LH Dash Fan toggle switches control the dash fans. To run a fan, reposition a switch from OFF to either HIGH or LOW for a desired speed. Speaker Select Switch The Speaker Select toggle switch controls the interior and exterior speakers of the public address (P.A.) system. [...] Insert the belt clip into the belt buckle. 5. Ensure the belt is retracted, locked, and snug. WHEELCHAIR SYSTEM Rev. A - 28/03/2008 Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* 11. INTRODUCTIONThis manual describes the operating features and safety equipment of the New Flyer transit vehicle. All personnel involved in the operation of the vehicle should be acquainted with this manual and should familiarize themselves with the vehicle, before providing any public service. Knowing the contents of this booklet and following its recommendations will help to assure safe and trouble-free operation. It is not the intention or responsibility of this manual to give instruction in the use of common sense, basic skills and rules of driving; therefore, it is assumed that you, the operator, are fully qualified to operate a public transit vehicle. This manual and any other supplied should be considered a permanent part of the vehicle and remain with the vehicle at all times. The information and specifications throughout this manual are up to date at time of publication. New Flyer reserves the right to change the content of this manual at any time without notice. Any malfunction which interferes with the safe operation of the vehicle should be reported immediately to the appropriate service personnel. \u261E NOTE:\_ New Flyer urges you the driver to read this publication carefully, as well as the following manuals which are readily available from the respective manufacturer.\_ \u2022\_ Voi th\_ Operating Instructions DIWA.5 Transmissions \u007F\_ Cummins \_ISL Series Engine Owner\u2019s Manual ![][image\_dgYbaQC4sI1Jb0G\u002BMggVPA==] \r\n\u003C/page\_7\u003E\r\n\u003Cpage\_8\u003E\r\n2\*\* Copyright \u00A9 2008 New Flyer Industries Canada ULC\*\* Rev.
